New Delhi, the 7th September. 1981 

S.O. 2569. —In pursuance of sub-section (3) of Section 26 
of the Monopolies & Restrictive Trade Practices Act, 1969 
(54 of 1969), the Central Government hereby notifies the 
cancellation of the registration of M]s. Cloth Traders Private 
Limited under the said Act (Certificate of Registration No. 
814|71). 


2. The company will, however, ensure that it may not take 
up any fresh activity which would falL within the scope of 
Sections 2(v) and 2(r) of the MRTP Act without the prior 
approval of the Government under section 22 of the MRTP 
Act, as it would amount to setting up a new undertaking 
within the meaning of said section of the MRTP Act.

without going into the merits of the case.”

&CL 2519.—It is hereby notified for general information that 
the institution mentioned'"below has been approved by the 
Secretary, Department of Science and Technology, New Delhi 
he piCMiibcd aulhoiity for the purposes of clause (ii) pf sub¬ 
notion (1) of section 35 of the Ineome-tax Act, ”#61 read 
with Rule 6(vi) of Income-tax Rules, 1962^. under the cate¬ 
gory ‘Association” m the areas of other natural and applied 
science, subject to the following conditions : 

(1) That the Natural Products Research Association, 
Bombay will maintain a separate account of sums received* 
by it for scientific research in the field of natural and appplied 
sciences other than agriculture/animal husbandry /fisheries and 
medicines; 

(2) That the said Association will furnish annual return of 
its scientific research activities to the prescribed authority 
for every financial year in such forms as may be laid down 
and intimated to them for this purpose by 30th April each 
year. 

(3) That the, said Association will submit the annual 
return and stat^mentjgf^Accounts to the Commissioner of 
Income-tax for ewtfyear.

S.O. 2521*—In continuation of this Depaitmenfs Notifica¬ 
tion No. 2708 <F. No, 203/18/79-ITA.II), dated 2-2-1979, it 
is heieby notified for general information that the institution 
mentioned below has been approved by Indian Council of 
Medical Research, New Delhi, ihe prescribed authority for 
thc purposes of clause (ii) of subjection (1) of Section 35 
of the Income-tax Act 1961 read With Rule 6(ii) of the 
Income-tax Rules, 1962 under the category of “scientific 
research association” in the field of medical leseaieh subject 
to the following conditions :— 

(i) That the Association will maintain a sepal ate account 
of the sums received by it for medical research. 

(ii) That the Association will furnish annual icturns of 

its scientific research activities to the Council by 
31st May each year hi the latest in such form as 
may be laid down mid intimated to them for this 
purpose. 

(iii) That thc Association will furnish an annual audited 

statement of accounts to the Council by 31#t 
May each year and in addition send a copy of it to 
the concerned Income-tax Commissioner. 

(iv) Vigorous efforts should be made to complete the 

construction work at Sakct and shift the activities 
of the Centre from Modinagar to the new site at 
Saket as early us possible, 

(v) Thc l&cnich activities should be intensified after 

constructing the building and lecrulting more scien¬ 
tific staff. 

(vi) No further extension will he allowed unless these 

two (iv Sc \) conditions are satisfied during this 
period of two yents (2-1-1981 to 1-1-1983) 

INSTITUTION 

Oujarmal Modi Hospital & Research Centre for Medical 
Sciences, New Delhi.

12-1-1981 to 1-1-1983).

the institution mentioned below has been approved by the

cribed authority for the purposes of clause (il) of subjection

category of “scientific research association" in the field of 
Medical Research subject to the following conditions :—

account of the sums received by it for medical re- 
research ,

it, scientific research activities to ihe Council by

may be laid down and Intimated to them for this 
purpose.

statement of accounts to the Council by 31st May,

S.O. 2560.—It is hcieby notified foi geneial infoimation 
that the institution mentioned below has been approved by 
Indian Council of Medical Research, New Delhi, the prescri¬ 
bed authority for the purposes of clause (ii) of sub-section 
(l) of Section 35 of the Income-tax Act, 1961 read with 
Rule 6(ii) of the Income-tax Rules, 1962 under the category 
of ‘^Scientific Research Association” in the field of Medical 
Rcseaich subject to the following conditions .— 

(i) That thc Association will maintain a separate ac¬ 
count of the sums received by it for medical re¬ 
search. 

(ii) That the Association will furnish annual return of 
its scientific research activities to the Council by 
31st May each year at the latest in such form as 
may be laid down and intimated to them for this 
purpose. 

(iii) That the Association will furnish a copy of the 
annual auditied statement of accounts to the Coun¬ 
cil by 31 st May each year and in addition send a 
copy of it to the concerned Income-tax Commis¬ 
sioner. 

INSTITUTION 

Sita Ram Bhaitia Institute of Scientific Research, New 
Delhi. 

The notification is effective for a period of three yean 
from 28-3-1981 to 27-3*1984.

for 6 Nos. TimJcan Front Bogie Axle Box Roller bearing 
etc. during April-78—March-79 licensing period.

cate of Exchange Control Purposes copy of the above 
licence on the ground that the Original Exchange Control 
Purposes copy has been lost without having been registered 
with the Customs Authorities and not utilized at all for remit¬ 
tance purposes. The license: agrees U return the Original

later, to this office for record.

signed is satisfied that the Origin'll Exchange Control 
Purposes copy of Import Licence No. G|R]3200978, dated 
24-1-1979 has bc?en lost and directs that duplicate copy of 
the Exchange Control Purposes Copy of the Tmpoit Licence

MINISTRY OF INDUSTRY 
(Department ol Industrial Development) 

New Delhi, the 9th September, 1981 

S.O. 2586.—In exercise of the powers conferred by sec¬ 
tion 6 of the Suit Cess Act, 1953 (49 of 1953), the Central 
Government hereby makes the following rules further to 
amend the Sail Cess Rules, 1964, nnmely :— 

1. These rules mty be called the Salt Cess (Amend¬ 

ment) Rules, 1981. 

2 . In rule 9 of the Salt Cess Rules, 1964, 

(i) for clause (a), the following clause shall be sub¬ 
sumed, namely :— 

“(a) Salt taken out of India to any country out¬ 
side India by sea and if the country is not 

connected by sea then by rail or road. 

.the whole”. 

(ii) clause (f) shall be omitted, 

[No. 0201117178-Salt] 

PURAN CHAND, Under Secy. 

Note.—The Salt Cess Rules, 1964 have already been 
amended vide S.O. No. 4037 dated l 7 th November, 
1964 S.O. No. 2038 dated 16th June 1965, S.O. 
No. 1519 dated 12th May 1966, S.O. No. 3502 
dated 18th November, 1066, a nd S.O. No. 2543 
dated 27th August 1973.

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM, CHEMICAL AND 
FERTILIZER 
(Department of Petroleum) 

New Delhi, the 16th September, 1981 

S.O. 2589.—Whereas by a notification of Government of 
India in the Ministry of Petroleum, Chemicals and Fertilizer 
(Department of Petroleum) S.O. 2050 dated 2-8-80 under 
Sub-scction (1) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land) Act, 1962 
(50 of 1962) the Central Government declared its intention 
to acquire the Right of User in the lands specified in the 
schedule appended to that notification for the purpose of 
laying pipeline; 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under Sub¬ 
section (1) of Section 6 of the said Act submitted report to 
the Government; 

And further the Central Government hag after considering 
tho said report, decided to acquire the right of user in the lands 
specified in the schedule appended to this notification; 

Now therefore in exercise of the power conferred by Sub- 
Section (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the 
said lands specified in the schedule appended to this notifi¬ 
cation hereby acquired for laying the pipelines; 

And further in exercise of the power confeired by Sub¬ 
jection (4) of that section, the Central Government directs 
that the right of user in the said lands shall instead of 
vesting in the Central Government vest on this date of the 
publication of this declaration in the Indian Oil Corporation 
Limited free from all encumbrances.

S.O* 2592,—In exercise of the powers conferred by the 
roviso to article 309 of the Constitution, the President here- 
y makes the following rules further to amend the Depart¬ 
ment of Sp$ce Employee*’ (Classification, Control and 

Appeal) Rules, 1976, namely :— 

1. (1) These Rules may be called the Department of Space 
/Employees’ (Classification, Control and Appeal) Amend¬ 
ment Rules, 1981. 

(2) They shall come into fore# on the date of their pub¬ 
lication In the Official Gazette, 

2. In the Department of Space Employees’ (Classification, 
Control and Appeal) Rules, 1976, in rule 7, to sub-rule (4), 
the following proviso shall be added, namely :— 

‘‘Provided that no such further inquiry shall be order¬ 
ed except in case when the penalty of dismissal, 
removal or compulsory retirement from service 
has been set aside by a Court of law on technical 
grounds without goin^ into the merit-9 of the case 
or when fresh material has come to light which 
wax not before the Court".

MINISTRY OF ENERGY 

(Department of Coal) 

New Delhi, the 19th September, 1981 

S.O. 2593.—Whereas in pursuance of the Notification of 
the Government of India in the Late Ministry of Mines and 
Fuel (Department of Mines and Fuel) No. S.O. 1334 dated 
the 24 th April, 1962, under Section 9 of tho Coal Bearing 
Areas (Acquisition and Development ) Act, 1957 (20 of 
1957), the Central Government has acquired 247.75 acres 
(approximately) or 100.30 hectares (approximately of land in 
villages Balanda and Nakhstrapur, Thana Colliery P.S., Dis¬ 
trict Dhepkanal (Orissa). 

And whereas Gundi Sahu ion of Bachia Sahu and Rangai 
Bewa daughter of Bachia Sahu of village Ealanda, P.O. 
Dera Colliery, District Dhenkanal (Orissa), the persons in¬ 
terested have not, under Section 13 of the said Act, preferred 
their claim for compensation for acquisition of their land 
measuring 0.02 acre or 0 01 hectare which forms part of 
land so acquired before the Competent Authority, 

And whereas the amount of compensation payable for the 
said acquisition, could not be fixed by agreement, there being 
a dispute as to the sufficiency of the amount of compensa¬ 
tion offered and the amount so offered, was accepted by the 
claimants under protest. 

Now therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub¬ 
section (2) of Section 14 of the said Act, the Central Govern¬ 
ment hereby constitutes a Tribunal consisting of the District 
Judge, Dhenkanal (Orissa), Part-Time Tribunal, for the pur¬ 
pose of determining (he amount of compensation payable to 
the claimants.

ferred their claim for compensation for acquisition of their

said acquisition, could not be fixed by agreement, there being

^.O, 2600.—Wherpag in pursuance of the Notification of 
the Government of India in the Late Ministry of Mines and 
Fuel (Department of Mines and Fuel) No. S.O. 1334 dated 
the. 24th April, 1962, under Section 9 of the Coal Bearing 
Areas (Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 
1957), the Central Government ha* acquiied 247.75 acres

S.O, 2616.—Whereas in pursuance of the Notification of the 
Government of India in the Late Ministry of Mines and Fuel, 
(Department of Mines and Fuel) No. S.O. 1334 dated the 24th 
April, 1962, under Section 9 of the Coal Boaiiug Areas 
(Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957), the 
central Government has acquired 247.75 acres (approximately) 
or 100.30 hectares (approximately) of land in villages Balanda 
and Nakhstrapur, Thana Colliery, P.S. District Dhenkanal 
(Orissa), 

And whereas, Most Kalika wife of Darbu Sahu of village 
Balanda, P.O. Dera Colliery, District Dhenkanal (Orissa), the 
person intersted has not, under Section 13 of the said Act, 
preferred his claim for compensation for acquisition of his 
land measuring 0,46 acre or 0.18 hectare which forms part 
of land so acquired before the Competent Authority. 

And whereas the amount of compensation payable for the 
said acquisition, could not be fixed by agreement, there being 
a dispute as to the sufficiency of the amount of compensation 
offered and the amount so offered, was accepted by the claim¬ 
ant under protest. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
Sub-section (2) of Section 14 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby constitutes a Tribunal consisting of the 
District Judge, Dhenkanal (Orissa), Part-Time Tribunal, for 
the purpose of determining the amount of compensation pay¬ 
able to the claimant.

S.O. 2619—Whereas in pursuance of the Notification of the 
Government of India in the Late Ministry of Mines and Fuel 
(Department of Mines and Fuel) No. S.O, 1334 dated the 24th 
April, 1962, under Section 9 of the Coal Bearing Areas 
(Acquisition and Development) Act, 1937 (20 of 1957), the 
Central Government has acquired 247.75 acres (approximately) 
or 100.30 hectares (oppioximalely) of land in villages Balanda 
and Nakhstrapur, Thana Colliery P.S., District DhenkanaJ 
(Orissa). 

And whereas, Madana Nayak son of Shii Dome Nayak of 
village Balanda, P. O. Dcra Colliery, District Dhenkanal 
(Orhsa), the peison interested has not, under Section 13 of 
the said Act, prefened his claim for compensation for acqui¬ 
sition of his land measuring 0 53 acre or 0,21 hectare which 
forms part of land so acquired before the Competent Authority. 

And whereas the amount of compensation payable for the 
said acquisition, could not be fixed by agreement, there being 
a dispute as to the sufficiency of the amount of compensation 
offered and the amount so offered, whs accepted by the 
claimant under protest. 


And whereas Paban Nayak S/o Saran Nayak, Mangta Nayak. 
Dwari Nayak and Bhikhari Nayak all eons of Gopal Nayak 
of village Balanda, P.O. Dera Colliery, District Dhcnkan«l 
(Orissa), the persons interested have not, under Section ]3 
of the said Act, preferred their claim for compensation for 
acquisition of their land measuring 0.54 acre or 0 22 hectare 
which forms part of land so acquired before the Competent 
Authority. 

And whereas the amount of compensation payable for 
the said acquisition, could not be fixed by agreement, there 
being a dispute as to the sufficiency of the arpount of com¬ 
ens alio n offered and the amount so offered, was accepted 
y the claimants under protest. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by Sub^ 
Section (2) of Section 14 of the said Act, the Central Govern¬ 
ment hereby constitutes a Tribunal consisting of the District 
Judge, Dhenkanal (Orissa), Part-Time Tribunal, for the pur¬

the claimants.

S-O. 2623.—Whereas in pursuance of the Notification of the 
Government of India in the Late Ministry of Mines and Fuel,

S.O. 2640, —-Whereas in pursuance of the Notification of 
the Government of India in th© Late Ministry of Mines and 
Fuel (Department of Mines and Fuel) No. S.O. 1334 dated 
the 24th April, 1962, under Section 9 of th© Coal Bearing 
Areas (Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 
1957), the Central Government has acquired 247.75 acres 
(approximately) or 100.30 hectares (approximately^ of land 
in tillages Baland and Nakhstrapur, Thana Coluery P.S. 
District. Dhenkanal (Orissa). 

And whereas, Maguni Nayak S/o Gati Nayak of village 
Balanda, P.O, Dera Colliery, District Dhenkanal (Orissa), 
the person interested has not, under Section 13 of the said 
Act preferred his claim for compensation for acquisition 
of his land measuring 1,31 acres or 0 53 hectare which forms 
part of land so acquired before the Competent Authority. 

And whereas, the amount of compensation payable for the 
said acquisition, could not be fixed by agreement, there 
being a dispute as to the sufficiency of the amount of com- 
ensalion offered and the amount so offered, was accepted 
y the claimant under protest. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
Sub-Section (2) of Section 14 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby constitutes a Tribunal consisting of the 
District Judge, Dhenkanal (Orissa), Part-Time Tribunal, 
ror the purpose of determining the amount of compensation 
payable to the Claimant.

S.O. 2648.-—-Whereas In persuance of the Notification of 
the Government of India in the Late Ministry of Mines and 
Fuel (Department of Mines and Fuel) No. S.O. 1334 dated 
the 24th April, 1962, under Section 9 of the Coal Rearing 
Areas (Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 
1957), the Central Government has acquired 247.75 acres 
(approximately) or 100.30 hectares (approximately) of land 
in villages Balanda a nd Nakhstrapur, Thana Colliery P.S., 
District Dhenkanal (Orissa). 

And whereas, Bagha Sahu, Banchha Sahu, Gaurang Sahu 
and Kestu Sahu all sons of Kanhai Sahu of village Balanda, 


P.O. Dora Colliery, District Dhenkanal (Orissa), the persona 
interested have not, under Section 13 of the said Act, pre¬ 
ferred their claim for compensation for acquisition of their 
land measuring 1.79 acres or 0.72 hectare which forms 
part of land so acquired before the Competent Authority. 

And whereas, the amount of compensation payable for 
the said acquisition, could not be fixed by agreement* there 
being a dispute as to the sufficiency of the amount of com¬ 
pensation offered and the amount so offered, was accepted 
by the claimants under protest. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
Sub-Section (2) of Section 14 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby constitutes a Tribunal consisting nf the 
District Judge. Dhenkanal (Orissa), Part-Time Tribunal, for 
the purpose of determining the amount of compensation payable 
to tho claimants.

S.O. 2657,—Whereas in pursuance of the Notification of 
tho Government of India in the Late Ministry of Mines and 
Fuel (Department of Mtn^s and Fuel) No, S.O. 1334 dated 
the 24th April, 1962, under Section 9 of the Coal Bearing 
Areas (Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 
1957), tho Central Government has acquired 247.75 acres 
( approximately) or 100.30 hectares (approximately) of land 
in villages Balanda and Nakhstrapur, Thena Colliery P. S.. 
District Dhrnkanal (Orissa), 

And whereas, Bita Pradhan son of Sashi Pradhan, 
Pandari Bewa w/o Sashi Pradhan and Dalimba Dei w|o 
Banu Behra (daughter of Sashi Pradhan) of village Balanda, 
B. O., Dera Colliery, District Dbenkannl (Orissa), the per¬ 
sons interested have not, under Section 13 of the said Act. 
preferred their claim for compensation for acquisition of 
their land measuring 2.91 acres or 1.18 hectares which 
forms part of land so acquired before the Competent 
Authority. 

And whereas the amount of compensation payable for the 
said acquisition, could not be fixed by agreement, there be¬ 
ing a dispute as to the sufficiency of the amount of com¬ 
pensation offered and the amount so offered, was accepted 
by the claimant under protest. 

Now therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
Sub-Section (2) of Section 14 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby constitutes a Tribunal consisting of the 
District Judge, Dhenkanal (Orissa) Part-Time Tribunal, for 
the purpose of determining the amount of compensation 
payable to the claimant.

MINISTRY OF AGRICULTURE 
(Department of Food) 

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 31st August, 1981 
S.O. 2672 «— Wheroas the Central Government has 
ceased to perform the functions of purchase, storage, move¬ 
ment, transport, disUibutior and sale of foodgrains dope by the 
Department of Food, the Regional Directorates of Food, the- 
ProcuTOient Directors and the P^y & Accounts Officer of the 
Department of Food which under Section 13 of Food Corpora¬ 
tions Act, 1964 07 of 1964) are the fu ictions of the Food Corpo¬ 
ration of India. 

And whereas the following officers and employees serving 
ip the Department of Food, the Regional Directorate of FOod, 
the Procurement Directorates and the Pay & Accounts Offices 
of the Department of Food and engaged in the performance 
of the functions mentioned above hive not, in response to the 
circular of the Central Gwt dated the 16th April, 1971 
intimitod, within the date specified therein, their intention of 
not becoming employees of lh; Food Corporation of India, 
as required by the p oviso to Sub Section 12A of the said Act; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by Sec¬ 
tion 12A of the Food Corporations Act, 1964 (37 of 1964) as 
amended upto date the Central Government hereby transfer 
the following officers & employees to the Food Corporation of 
India with effect from the date mentioned against each of them

MINISTRY OF WORKS & HOUSING 

New Delhi, the 10th September, 1981 

S.O. 2678. —Whereas certain modifications, which the Cen¬ 
tral Government proposes to make in the Master Plan for 
Delhi regarding the area mentioned hereunder, were published 
by the Delhi Development Authority with Notice No. 
F. 20(12)179 M.P. dated 25-10-80, in accordance with the 
provisions of section 44 of the Delhi Development Act, 
1957 (61 of 1957) inviting objcQtions/suggestions, as 

required by sub-section (3) of section 11A of the said 
Act, within thirty days from the date of said notice ; 

And whereas no objection or suggestion has been received 
with regard to the aforesaid modifications; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (2) of section 11A of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby makes the following modifications in 
the said Master Plan for Delhi with effect from the date of 
publication of this notification in the Gazette of India, 
namely :— 

MODIFICATIONS 

(i) u Th© land use of an area known as plot No, 115 

measuring 3.66 hectares (9.00 acres), falling in 
Zone D-9 (Central Vista Zone), bounded by 36.57 M. 
wide Pandit Pant Marg on the west, Lok Sabba 
Bhawan on the south-east and 60.96 M. wide Tal- 
katora Road on the east, is changed from ‘Govern¬ 
ment and semi-government offices’ to ‘Recreation.’ 

(ii) The land us© of an area known as Plot No. 36 

measuring 3.76 hectares (9.25 acres), falling in 
Zone D-9 (Central Vista Zone) and bounded by 
18.25 M. road on the north-west, 18.68 M. road on 
the south-east and 30,48 M. Hastings Road on the 
east, is changed from ‘Government and semi-govern¬ 
ment offices 1 to ‘Recreation.*

MINISTRY OF RURAL RECONSTRUCTION 

New Delhi, the 18th September, 1981 

S.O, 2679.—Whereas a draft of the Bread Wheat Flooi 
Grading and Marking Rules, 1980, was published, as re¬ 
quired by section 3 of the Agricultural Produce (Grading 
and Marking) Act, 1937 (1 of 1937), on pages 2644 to 
2648 of the Gazette of India, Part IT, Section 3, Sub-section 
(ii), dated the 2nd August, 1980 under the notification of 
the Government of India in the Ministry of Rural Recons¬ 
truction No. S. O. 2054, dated the 11th July, 19&0, invit¬ 
ing objections and suggestion# from all persons likely to be 
-affected thereby before the expiry of the period of fortyfite 
days from the date of publication of the said notification in 
the Official Gazette; 

And whereas the copies of the said Gazette were made 
available to the public on the 18th August, 1980; 

And whereas objection! and suggestions received in res¬ 
pect of the said draft have been considered by the Central 
Government: 

Now, therefore, in exercise of th# powers conferred by 
section 3 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby 
makes the following rules, namely ; 

1* Short title, application and commencement.—(1) 
These rules may be called the Bread Wheat Floor Grading 
and Marking Rules, 1981. 

(2) They shall apply to Bread Wheat Floor produced in 
India. 

(3) They shall come into force on the dat* pf their publi¬ 
cation in the Official Gazette. 

2. Definitions.—In these, rules, unless the context other¬ 
wise requires,— 

(1) “Agricultural Marketing Adviser*’ means the Agricul¬ 
tural Marketing Adviser to the Government of India, 

(2) “Authorised packer" means a person or a body of 
persons who has been granted a certificate of authorisation 
under rule 3 of the General Grading and Marking Rules, 
1937, in relation to Bread Wheat Flour. 

(3) “Schedule” means a Schedule appended to theee rules. 

3. Grade designations.—The grade designation to indi¬ 
cate the quality of Bread Wheat Flour ahall be as set out in 
column 1 of Schedule L 

4. Definition of quality.^The quality indicated by the 
grade designation shall be as set out against the said designa¬ 
tion in columns 2 to 13 of Schedule I* 

5. Grade designation mark.—The grade designation mark 
shall consist of a label supplied by the Agricultural Mar¬ 
keting Adviser specifying the grade designation and bear¬ 
ing a design consisting of an outline map of India with the 
word “AGMARK" and the figure of the rising sun with the 


words “Produce of India" and resembling the 

mark set out in Schedule R. 

NOTE : (1) The grade designation mark to be used 
on paper or cloth bags shall consist of a paste-on 
label specifying the grade designation, 

(2) The grad© designation mark to bo used on B-twill 
jute bags shall consist of a rectangular tie-on label 
specifying the grade designation. 

6 . Method of marking ; (1) The grade designation mark 
shall be securely affixed on each container in a manner as 
approved by the Agricultural Marketing Adviser and shalD 
clearly show the following particulars, namely : 

(a) date of packing; 

(b) lot number (batch [code ; 

(c) name and address of packer, and 

(d) net weight 

(2) In addition to the grade designation mark, the follow¬ 
ing particulars shall also be marked on each label : 

(a) date of packing; 

(b) lot pumber)batch)codo; 

(b) name* and address of packer, and 

(<!)r net weight. 

(3) The sale price shall bo clearly marked oh each con¬ 
tainer. 

(4) An authorised packer may, after obtaining the prior 
approval of the Agricultural Marketing Adviser, mark his 
private trade mark on a container in a manner approved by 
the said officer, provided the private trad# mark does not 
represent quality or grad* of the Bread Wheat Flour differ¬ 
ent from that indicated by the grhde designation mark 
affixed on the container in accordance with these rules. 

7. Method of packing : (1) Only sound* clean and dry 
container made of paper, cloth, B-twlll jute or any other 
material as may be approved by the Agricultural Market¬ 
ing Adviser shall be used for packing. 

(2) The container shall be free from any insect infestation 
or fungus contamination and also free from any undesir¬ 
able smell. 

(3) Each package shall be securely closed and sealed in 
the manner prescribed by the Agricultural Marketing Adviser. 

(4) Each package shall contain Bread Wheat Flour of 
one grade designation only. 

(5) The net weight of Bread Wheat Flour in each con¬ 
tainer shall be 50 gms., 100 gms., 200 gmu., 400 gms., 
500 gms., t kgs.. 2 kgs., 5 kgs, and thereafter in multiples of 

5 kg«

New Delhi, the I9lh September, 1981 

S.O. 2687.—In puisuanec of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 q£ 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the following nwaid of the Cenhal Govt. 
Industrial Tribunal, Jubalpui in the indusliial dispute between 
the employers in relation to the management of Chandametta 
Colliery of Western Coalfields Limited, Pench Aiea Chan’ 
dametia Group, Disll. Chhindwara (M.P. and thcii woik- 
men, which was received by the Cenl.al Government on the 
9th September, 1981. 

BEFORE JUSTICE SHRI S. 1C YYAS, PRESIDING 

OFFICER, CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUS I RIAL 

TRIBUN AL-CUM-LABOUR COURT JAB VLPUR (M.P 1 
Cose No, CGIT|LC(R)l7)|197* 

PARTIES ; 

Lmployers in relation to the Management of Ch.mda- 
metta Colliciy of Western Coulticlds 1 imited, 
Chandametta and their w'orkmcn repit'senletl through 
ihe Bhartiya Koyla Lhvdan Mazdoo.* Sangh, P,0 
Chandametta, Distiict Chhmdw'ua (M.P.). 

APPEARANCEvS ; 

For Union—Shri S, S. Shakarwar, /Advocate. 

For Management.—Shri P. S. Nair, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY : Coal DISTRICT ; Chhindu.ua (M.P.) 

AWARD 

In exercise of the poweis confcncd by Clause 10<I)(d) of 
the Industrial Disputes Act 1947 Government of India 
in the Ministry of Labour has referred the following dispute 
to this Tribunal vide Notification No. L-22013(2)|77-D.1V(A) 
dated 14-2-1978, for adjudication :— 

Whether the action of the management of Western Coal¬ 
fields f.imited. Pench Area, Chandametta Group 
(in relation to Chandametta Colliery) in dismissing 
Shu D. N. Tnputi, Sirdar, with elFecl from 21xl 
January, 1975, is (justified ? If not, to what iclicf 
is the concerned woihmnn entitled ? 

2. From the statements of claim filed by die paitic* .md 
(heir rejoinders ihc facts giving use io (his jefeiencc may be 
stated as under :—I 

The workman Shri D N. IT [palh» was working as a 
Mining Sirdar in the Chandametta Colliery of the Western 
Coalfields Limited from 25-3-1974. A chaigesheet was 
served on him in which it was, intei alia, alleged that on the 
day he remained ahscnL from duty he insugitcd the other work¬ 
men to remain absent from work, that he was remaining 
absent as a matter of habit, that he was found dmnk and 
guilty of riotous bchavium, that he was guilty of wilful insu¬ 
bordination, that he threatened other workers and subjected 
them to physical violence, that he was paiticipating in an 
illegal stiike and instigating other workers also to join illegal 
strike and that he was thus guilty for committing breach of 
the xelevant provisions of the Mines Act and Industrial Dis- 
putes Act. On such a charge-sheet being served on the work¬ 
man an enquiry was held, At the conclusion of the enquiry 
the Enquiry Officer found the workman guilty. Ihc mana¬ 
gement accordingly accepted the report of the Enquiry Offi¬ 
cer and by an order dated 17-1-1975 the workman was dis¬ 
missed from service. The order of dismissal is the subject 
of the present reference. 

3. in statement of claim the woikman has alleged Thai 
because of his keen interest in union activities the manage¬ 
ment was hostile to him, that he wn* not guilty of any of 
the charges for which An enquiiy was held, that the enquiry 
held £igainst him was bad in law and (hat the punishment 
awarded to him is not proportionin' to the findings of the 
Enquiry Officer, 

4. ' According to the management, the workman was pro¬ 
perty chprge-sheeted and there was a valid enquiry on the 
aforesaid charges. Objections taken with icgard to the mode 
of enquiry were denied by Ihc' management. It was also 
stated that since the workman was holding the .statutory post 
of a Mining Sirdar he vvus expected to obey and not violate 
the provisions cf law. Tt is lastly urged that in view of the 
findings of the Enquiiy Officer that the workman whs guilty 
Of misconduct the punishment awarded was fully justified. 


[Part II —Sec. 3(ii)J 

5. On thi aforesaid picas of both the parties the following 
issues were framed :— 

1. Whether the woikman was transferred with ulterior 

motives on account of union activities? 

2. Whether the domestic enquiry was piopei and full 

opportunity was granted to the delinquent work¬ 
man ? 

3. Whether the Enquiry Officer showed undue fuvoui to 

the management by granting adjournment so as to 
suit the management in the matter of preparing 
witnesses ? 

4. Whether second show cause notice was given, if not 

the effect ? 

5. Whether the punishment awaided was piopei ? 

6. Whethei the Enquiry Officei was biased ? 

7. Whether the employer has lost confidence in the emp 

Icjyec ? 

8. \\ he the j Shi i Tiiputhi is a workman and the i de¬ 

fence is valid?? 

9. Relief and costs 7 

6 Gpi cf the afoiesaid issues, fesuc Nos. 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 
were considered an I decided vide Older passed by my learn¬ 
ed picdece'-xoi on December J9, 1980. It was held that the 
domestic enquiiy was legal and proper; that the enquiry was 
conduced in adherence to the piinciplc of natural justice, 
that the Enquiry Officer was not biased against the delin¬ 
quent workman and that the second show cause notice was 
not recesTuy before awarding the punishment. 

7. After the aforesaid findings on the aforesaid issues weie 
lecoidcd the case was fixed lor the evidence on the remain* 
ing issues. No oral evidence was thereafter led by the parties 
and final arguments were heard on (be basis of the oral 
ard documentary evidence already on lecoid. As already 
stated a hove, findings weie reserved on Issues Nos. 1, 5, 7 
and 9. I have considered the contentions of both the patties 
in the light of the material on lecord. My findings on the 
aloiesaid issues are as under ;— 

Tssuc No. 1 —Thcie was no uJleiioi molive in transfer! jng 
lac woikman liom the one incline to another incline in the 
same mine. 

Issue No. 5—The punishment awarded is pioper. 

Issue No. 7.—As the punishment awaided to the workman 
ha* been found to be proper no finding on this issue is ncree- 
saiy. 

Issue No, 9.—The workman concerned is not entitled tc 
any relief. 

Reasons for the afoiesaid findings ; 

Dsiie No. 1 ,—It U evident on the documentalv as well 
as oral evidence that the workman was not transfeued from 
otie mine at one place to the other mine at other plate. 
But he was allotted duty from on* incline lo another incline 
in the .same mine. The management has full powers Lo allot 
duties to their workmen and unless the workman is trans¬ 
ferred from one station lo the other station such a change 
in the allotment of duties would not amount to transfer 
If the change of duty from one place to another place in the 
same mine does not amount to tiansfcr the question of ul¬ 
terior motive on the part of the management docs not arise. 

I thercfoie, hold that there was neither any transfer nor any 
question of ulterior motive on the part of ths management 
in directing a change in the place of work of the workman 
Shri D. N\ TripathJ. Issue No. 1 is accordingly found 
against ihe workman. 

Issue No. 5.—It was u’gcd on behalf of the workman that 
the major punishment of dismissal from service was not

hereby publishes the following award of the Central Govern¬ 
ment Industrial Tribunal Calcutta, m the industrial dispute 
between the employers m relation to the management of Pur- 
binchal Bank Limited, and then workman, which was lecelved 
hy the Central Government on the 5 9 81 

BEFORE MR TLJSTICE R BHATTACHARYA, MA, 

B L , PRESIDING OFFICER, CENTRAL GOVERNMENT 
INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL AT CALCUTTA 
Reference No 75 of 1978 

PARTIES 

Tmployeis in relation to the management of thc Purban- 
ch il Bank Limited, Gauhnti 
AND 

Thier Workmen 



3326 HIE GAZhl IE OF INDIA : OCfOBhR 3, 19K1/ASV1NA 11, 1903 [Part 11— Sec. 3(h)] 


APPEARANCES : 

On behalf of Employe)b —Mi. P. C, Dekn, Advocate 
(Later on retired). 

On behalf of Workmen—Mr, D, N. Choudhury, Ad\o- 
cate, with Mr, H, N. Surma, Advocate, 

STATE: Assam, INDUSTRY; Rankins, 

AWARD 

This is a reference under Section 10 ol the IndusUial Dis¬ 
putes Act, 1947 sent by the Central Government to this Tri¬ 
bunal for adjudication of an industrial dispute existing be¬ 
tween the employers in relation to the management of Pur- 
banchal Rank Ltd., Gauhuti and their workmen by an Outer 
No. L-12012/46/78-D.1I,A djted 23id/28th August, 19 78. 
The dispute in question has been mentioned m the schedule to 
the order of reference in the following terms ; 

“Whether the action of the management of the Pur ban¬ 
chal Bank Limited, Gauhati in dismissing Shri 
Kamalakshya Las, Grade II Officer Nowgong Branch 
of the Bank with effect fiom 208-77 is legal and 
justified? If not, to what lelief is the workman 
concerned entitled V 

A copy of this reference has been sent to the General Man a 
ger, Purbanchal Bank Limited, Ulubari, Gauhati, hereinafter 
referred to as the “Bank” and lo the concerned workman 
Kamalakshya Das, Clearly there is no dispute that this 
reference has been made icad with Section 2A of the Indust¬ 
rial Disputes Act, 1947. The workman concrened appeared 
in this case and has been contesting against the Bank. 

2, In this case* both th© Bank and the workman have filed 
their respective written statement. The workman in this case, 
as stated earlier in th© order dated 7-4-81 disposing of a 
preliminaiy issue regarding the validity of the domestic en¬ 
quiry hold against the workman, wag dismissed on the gionnd 
of alleged misconduct after service of a charge-sheet and on 
th© basis of the finding arrived at by the Enquiry Officer or 
the materials collected at the domestic enquiry. It appears 
from the records that at the instance of the Bank two preli¬ 
minary issues were raised. The first issue was whether 
Kamalakshya Das involved in this case was a ‘workman’ 
under the indusrtial Disputes Act and the second preliminary 
point was whether the domestic enquiry was legal and proper 
and whether the findings of the Enquiry Officer were based on 
evidence or perverse. The first preliminary point was decided 
by an order dated 19th September, 1979 and the second pre¬ 
liminary point as already staled was passed on 7th April, 
1981. The cage is long pending. It was started in September, 
1978. Ultimately 19th and 20th of this month was fixed for 
peremptory hearing, Due to the action of the Bank the case 
could not be heard on 19th and 20th but ultimately, as agreed 
to by both the parties, 21st August, 1981 was fixed for 
hearing. When the case was taken up for hearing on 21st 
August the learned Advocate on behalf of the Bank appeared 
and submitted that he had instructions not lo appear and 
retired. The circumstances and how this case proceeded will 
appear in the order passed on 19-8-1981 and 20-8-1981. How¬ 
ever, when the Bank did not appear to contest on 21st August, 
1981, the case was taken up for ex-partc hearing. The con¬ 
cerned workman Kamalakshya Das examined himself. Two 
witnesses who were cited hv the Bank to give evidence in 
this case being present in the Tribunal on that day were 
examined by the Tribunal as its* witnesses for ends of Iffsticc 
and for coming to a proper and just decision. Mr. Choudhury, 
teAmed Advocate assisted by Mr. H, N. Sarmn leained Advo¬ 
cate appeared on behalf of the workman. The two prelimi¬ 
nary points raised earlier by the Bank were decided against 
the Bank. It was first held that Kamalakshya Das was a 
workman under the provisions of the Industrial Disputes Act 
and that the domestic enquiry held by the Bank was improper 
and illegal and that Kamalakshya Das did not get any pro¬ 
per opportunity for his self-defence. It. was further held 
that the findings of the Enquiry Officer in respect of Kamalak¬ 
shya was were biased and pcrveise. After this finding further 
hearing was necessary for the decision of the Tribunal whether

6 . Let me now considei the evidence and eiteumstances to 
see how far tne allegation* in the chaige-sheet against Kama¬ 
lakshya Das have been proved and whether he could be held 
guilty or any of the chaiges. Ihe evidence shows that one 
Meher All has a current account with the Bank. The account 
is inoperative foi several years and the balance lying in the 
account is ie^s than Ks. 100, that is much below Rs. 3000. 
ihe evidence snows that on 8-12-76 a cheque purported to 
have been issued by Meher Ali for an amount of Rs. 3000 
was presented at the Bank and ffiat the said cheque was 
passed and ultimately it was cashed, although there was not 
sufficient money in the account of Meher Ali, The evidence 
further'is that the cheque which was presented was a loose 
cheque and not such cheque which was supplied to Meher 
Ali the account holder. Further evidence is that when the 
fraud was "detecred an attempt was made to get the cheque 
in question but it was faund missing from the day’s voucher 
bundle. I have already mentioned the procedure as to how 
when a cheque is presented at the Bank, it is ultimately 
cashed. At first the cheque is piesented to the ledger keeper. 
There is no evidence who was the person presenting the che¬ 
que to the ledger keeper. Then it was passed on to the 
Officer who was to verify the signature of the account holder 
appearing on the cheque witn hi s specimen signature kent in 
the Bank and also if the amount stated in the cheque lying 
in the account in question. There is no evidence that Kama¬ 
lakshya Das verified the cheque. Then the cheque must have 
gone to the Officer who passed it for payment The evidence 
further shows that different persons passed the cheque and 
not Kamalakshya. After the cheque was passed for payment , 
it was sent for entry m the scroll book and on the day of 
occurrence, namely 8-12-76, the said cheque came to Kama¬ 
lakshya Das and Kamalakshya Das made the entry in the 
scroll book. The evidence further shows that his simple 
duty was to make the entry with reference to the cheque 
and nothing else. After the said entry in the scroll book 
was made, the evidence shows that the cheque must have 
gone to the ledger keeper and from ledger keeper it 
was sent to the cashier for payment who before payment 
enters the particulars of the cheque in the cashier scroll. 
The cashier who made the payment is still in service but 
has not been examined to say who received the payment from 
him. It should be remembered Jhat the Officer who passed 
the cheque was to give his signature in the cheque passing 
register. Kamalakshya Das has slated that the said cheque 
passing register with some relevant documents was produced 
at the domestic enquiry but except in thn scroll book where 
there was the entry in the handwriting of Kamalakshya in 
no other document was there any writing or signature of the 
charge-sheeted person, namely Kamalakshya Das. The evi¬ 
dence is that in the cheque passing register there was the 
mention of the disputed cheque but there is no signature 
against that entry although the Passing Officer was to give 
his own signature Kamlakshya has stated in this evidence 
quite cleaily that on that day he passed some other cheques 
along with another Officer and he has also asserted that the 
disputed cheque had rot been passed by him. In order to 
find Kamalakshya Das guilty of the charges framed against 
him, we are to see what part or parts Kamlakshya played 
in the process starting from the presentation of the cheque 
to the encashment of the cheque as already mentioned.

came to him, he with reference to the cbetnie already passed 
made the entry in the scroll book. Th~ concerned workman 
did nothing else in the process leading to the encashment. 
For the purpose of making entry in the scroll, book or in

connection with that Kamalakshya had no reason or occasion

case although the Bank had sufficient opportunity to contest 
and adduce evidence yet it did not avail itself of the oppor¬

my orders already passed that the Bank wanted to drag on 
the proceedings and wanted adjournment by making misre*

favour of the bank and that there is no witness to implicate

in the chargesheet. Clearly, theretorc, I have reason to hold 
that the bank has not come forward to contest for want of 
evidence. However, whatever that may be, r hold (hat thcr* 
is no evidence or ciicumstanees to prove the allegations against. 
Kamalakshya Dais and that the charges levelled against him 
have not at alt been proved. I have no nr,inner of doubt 
that Kamalakshya Das is quite innocent of all the allegations 
and charges made against him, 

8, Therefore, the order of dismissal passed against Kama¬ 
lakshya Das is highly illegal,^ unreasonabI * and unjustified. 
The said order of dismissal passed by the bank against him 
is hereby set aside and consequently Kamalakshya Das is 
deemed to be still in service and consequently he is entitled 
to get all arrears of wages and other service benefits due to 
him. Within a month from the publication of this awaid, 
Kamalakshya Das shall report for duty at the Bank and the 
Bank shall allow him to work.

4, Section 2fs) of the Industrial Disputes Act deflnies ‘work¬ 
man to mean any person (including an appi entice) employ¬

super visor y, technical or cleiical work for hire or reward, 
whether the terms of employment be expressed or implied,

has been dismissed, discharged or retienched in connection 
with, or as a consequence oL that dispute, ot whose dismissal, 
discharge or rettenchment has led to that dispute, but does 
not include any such person— 

(ij 'i*** 

(ii) *+* 

(ni) who is employed mainly in a managerial ot adminis- 
tiativc capacity r or 

(iv) who, being employed in a supervisory capacity, diaws 
wages exceeding five hundred rupees per mensem oi 
exercises, either by the nature of the duties attached 
to the office or by icason of the powers vested in 
him, functions mainly of a managerial nature/

6. Prima facie an Tndusrtial Tribunal ought to proceed on 
the basis that a reference made under Section 10 of the 
Indusrlial Disputes Act by the appropriate Government is in 
order. Tt is for those who allege that the reference is in¬ 
competent to satisfy the Tribunal that it is sc. The onus 
is therefore on the management to establish that the concerned 
workman is not a 'workman 1 in the contemplation of the 
Industrial Disputes Act and the reference is thcreiorc incom¬ 
petent. Apart from the question of*onus, it will be necessary 
to examine whether on the evidence placed before the Tri¬ 
bunal it is legitimate to hold that the duties of ihe concerned 
workman were of a supervisory nature. In the written state¬ 
ment filed on behalf of the management it is not pleaded that 
the concerned workman was cmnioycj in a supcivkoiy capa¬ 
city diuwin^ wages exceeding Ns. *>00 per mensem, Whn! is 
pleaded in parngianh 4 is th.it ho used lo peiforvii managerial 
functions dining the relevant period and the relViciue mad- 
on the wiono assumption that Si i Das was a woikman is 


: aptjin: 3, issi/srrfim 11 , 1903 3329 


entirely wrong and the Tribunal should therefore refuse to 
adjudicate upon a leteience which is not ail industrial dispute 
within the meaning of law. Ihe pleading is therefore at 
variance with the case sought to be made out at the hearing. 
If a strict view of the pleadings is to be taken, the manage¬ 
ment ought not to be allowed to travel beyond their plead¬ 
ings and^ allege that the concerned workman was employed in 
a supervisory capacity drawing wages exceeding Rs. 500 per 
mensem and is, therefore, not a. workman. 1 do not propose to 
take too technical a view oi the pleadings and do not object to 
the management to adduce evidence on the nature of duties 
of the concerned workman at (he lelevant point of time. 
It is strange that nobody who had any peisonal knowledge 
of the nature of duties of the concerned woikman came for- 
waid to give evidence. Any superior officer or even any 
colleague or a contemporary employee could have given evi¬ 
dence on ihe relevant question but no such pet son deposed at 
the hearing. The Chief Accountant* Shri B. K. Dhar, who 
gave evidence joined the service of the bank long after 
the relevant date at a time when the concerned work¬ 
man was not in employment of the Hank. There is not 
a scrap of documentary evidence from which it could 
he established that the concerned workman was em¬ 
ployed in a supervisory capacity. The Chief Accountant 
frankly admitted that he had no person knowledge 
of the natuie of duties of the concerned workman at the 
time of dismissal nor could he relv on any document from 
which those duties could be shown. The little evidence that 
he gave was based on sui mixes. He though! that the con¬ 
cerned workman was working in the Branch Coiitro’ Depai t- 
ment of the Head Office. 1 his was categorically denied by 
the concerned woikman. Ho concluded by saying that he 
had no personal knowledge as to whether Sri Knmnlukshyn 
Das ever discharged any supervisoiy duty. The oulci Of 
promotion also does not disclose that the concerned workman 
was employed in supervisory capacity, The only other peison 
who deposed at the hearing was Sri Kamataksbya Das Ihe 
concerned woikman himself. I-lc categorically stated that he 
had no supervisory duty. He was not transferred to Now f - 
pong but. continued to work in the Gauhati Office discharging 
the same duties after promotion as he had done before, During 
the very short time he was at the Head Office no work was 
allotted to him. Even the role he played in passing cheques 
was not of a supervisory character. The tally in v of signatures 
and checking of the balance were done by others. He merely 
put bis signature for payment. 

7. Having regard to the evidence which has been made 
available to the Tribunal, I am constrained to hold that there 
is no material on which the Tribunal can hold that the 
concerned workman was employed in a supervisory capacity. 
The fact that he was a Junior Officer, Grade TT by itself 
docs not establish that his duties were of supervisory nature. 

8. Learned advocate appearing on behalf of the concerned 
woikman relied on the decision of the Supreme Court in 
Ananda Bazar Palrikn (Private) Ltd., and Its workmen, 1969 
JI L.L.J. p. 670, where it has been held that: 

‘The principle which should be followed in deciding the 
question whether a person is employed in a super¬ 
visory capacity or on clerical work is that if a per¬ 
son is mainly doing supervisory work but incident¬ 
ally or for a fraction of the time also does some 
clerical work, it would have to be held that he is 
employed in supervisory capacity, and conversely if 
the main work done is of clerical nature, the mere 
fact that some supervisory duties are also carried 
out incidentally or as a small fraction of the work 
done by him will not convert his employment as a 
clerk into one in supervisory capacity,*’ 

Tn 4he facts and ciicumstances of the case wdth which T am 
concerned, there is nothing to indicate that Ihe concerned 
workman was employed in supervisory capacily or that his 
duties were of a supervisory nature. I hold on the basis of 
the evidence I have, that Sri K.imilakxhva Das, the concerned 
workman, is a “workman’* ir> the contemplation of Subjection 
fs) of Section 2 of the Tndusrtial Disputes \ct, 1947. 

9. Apart from the issue which 1 have answeicd, learned 
advocate on behalf .of Ihe Bank raised another issue on the 
basis of the pleadings, namely, that the present refcienee is 
incompetent, as it was made bv the Central Government in 
exercise of tile poweis conFened bv clause r d) of Subjection 



3330 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : OCTOBER 3, 1981/ASVINA 11, 1903 [Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 


(l) of Section 10 of the Indusrti.il Disputes Act, 1947 and 
not under the said provision read with Section 2A qf the 
Indusrtial Disputes Act, the contention being that as the 
dispute is concerned with the dismissal of an individual work¬ 
man it can be referred to the Tribunal for adjudication only 
having regard to the provisions contained in Section 2A of 
the Indusrtial Disputes Act. In my opinion there is no 
substance in this contention. Power to refer an indusrtial 
dispute to the Indusrtial Tribunal ri conferred by Section 
10 and not by Section 2A. Section 2A merely provides that 
a dispute relating to discharge, dismissal, retrenchment or 
termination of the services of an individual workman shall be 
deemed to be an industrial dispute notwithstanding that no 
other workman nor any union of workmen is a party to the 
dispute. So long as the provisions of Section 2A ate satis¬ 
fied, the mere fact that the order of Reference docs not allude 
to Section 2A can hardly be a ground for rejecting a reference 
as incompetent. The contention of the learned advocate 
therefore fails. 

10 . A date will be fixed for further hearing of the refer¬ 
ence in due course.

the properiety and legality of the domestic enquiry and it 
has been alleged that the enquiry was illegal, that he did not 
get any opportunity for self-defence and the report was bia¬

the matter of wihdrawal of the amount stated. At the request 
of the management of the Bank the issue as to whether 
the domestic enquiry was legal and proper was heard and 
the management led evidence in that respect. The workman 
did not however examine any witness. 

4, The question before me, at present, is whether or not 
the domestic enquiry held by the Bank was legal and proper, 
whether the delinquent was given proper opportunity for 
his self-defence and whether the report of the Enquiry Offi¬ 
cer finding the concerned workman guifiy i*, based upon prop¬ 
er evidence and to be accepted,

hts side.

ORDER 

Dated, 7th April, 1981 

In this reference under Section 10 of the Industrial Dis¬ 
putes Act, the dispute relates to the dismissal of the concern¬ 
ed workman. Kamalakshya Das, Grade il Officer, Nowgong 
branch of the Purbanchal Bank Limited, Guuhali. The Pur¬ 
banchal Bank Limited, Gauhati, hereinafter referred to as 
the “Bank’, and the concerned workman appeared and filed 
their respective written statement. A preliminary point was 
raised from the side of the. Bank as to whether the present 
dispute was an industrial dispute and whether Kamalakshya 
Das was a workman according to the definition of the 
Industrial Disputes Act. This Tribunal held by its Order dated 
19th September, 1979 that Kamalakshya Das was a work¬ 
man and that the dispute in question is an industrial dip 
putc. 

2. In this case the Bank’s allegation, in short, is that 
the concerned workman along with some other employees 
entered into a criminal conspiracy to defraud the bank and 
misappropriated a sum of Rs. 3,000 bv making a false credit 
entry in the account of a constituent of the Bank named 
Mehcr Ali and withdrew the said amount fraudulently. A 
preliminary enquiry was held and on the report o* the said

against all the dciinquests and held it domestic enquiry after 
giving all opportunities to the delinquents for self-defence. 
The Fnquiry Officer on consideration of the evidence collec¬

others guilty of the charge. The management on consideration 
of the said report found the concerned workman and others 
guilty and gave another chance to the delinquents to show' 
cause as to the ptoposed punishment. After second show 
cause notice and on consideiation of ihc case of the 
delinquent the management dismissed the delinquents as 
mentioned including Kamalakshya Das. 


7. The first contention on behalf of the workman at this

it has been argued by Mr. Choudhury that at the domestic

to the delinquent, the Enquiry Officer took certain documents 
for his consideiation. No copy of those documents were serv¬ 
ed on Kamalakshya Das and us a result thereof he could 
not properly cross-examine the witnesses examined on the s’de 
of the Bank. 

8 . One D, K. Sharma, the Law Officer of the Bank was 
the Enquiry Officer. He examined at the domestic cnquiiy 
two witnesses—(i) Jaladhar Das who was the Development 
Officer of the Bank and who conducted the preliminary en¬

the Agent of the Banks Gauhatl branch, Jaladhar while 
giving evidence before the Enquiry Officer produced his 
report which was marked Ext. 1 at the domestic enquiry. 
Ext, 2 was the statements of a co-delinquent Hiren Bora. 
It appears that two statements were recorded bv Jaladhar 
during preliminary enquiry and they were marked Ext, 2. 
There were other documents which were seen by Jaladhar 
during preliminary enquiry and they were also marked exhibits 
at the domestic enquiry. It will appear that alhough Jaladhar 
was personally examined at the domestic enquiry, he did not

save and except that hr made an entry in the form of a 
scroll. With regard to this document the Enquiry Officer was 
cross-examined before me. He has admitted tliHt at the time 
of domestic enquiry Ext. 2 meaning the statements of aco- 
delinquent made at the preliminary enquiry were submitted.

has referred to them. The Fnquiry Officer has stated that he 
does not remember if any copy of the statement wns given 
to the delinquent. This is an evasive reply. He says that he 
did not consider Ext. 2 for his report and he does not 
remember if he read Ext. 2 although the said statement will 
appear in the records of the proceedings held by him. He 
then says that he made those documents exhibits because he 
thought they might help him in his enquiry. The report of 
the preliminary enquiry held by Jaladhar was marked Exhibit 
before the Enquiry Officer. He has admitted that he considered 
this report, Ext. 1, He has admitted lhat he did not snpplv

not rely upon the repoit of the pi61iminary enquiry. He has

the time of .enquiry. As T have already stated, in the records 
of the enquiry held bv D. K. Shaima \vifi appear all the

Admittedly. Kamalakshya Das did not <?ct anv notice what¬

prrliminnty enquiry, Moi cover as D, K Sharma was npnoint- 
nd lo hold domestic enquiry independently he should not 
hnvp conside'ed the report of the preliminary cnqiihv and

AWARD 

Dated September 7, 1981 

By Notification No. L-12012/84/79TY1I.A dated 23rd 
August, 1980, Government of India in the Ministry of 
Labour has inferred the fullowing dispute to thin Tribunal, 
tor adjudication :— 

“Whethei the action of the management of Stale Bank 
of India, Bilaspur in dismissing from service, Shri 
Banwari Lai Soni, Cashicr-cum-Clerk, State Bank 
of India, Bilaspur from 5-4-1972 is justified 7 If 
not, to what lelief is the workman concerned 
entitled 7” 

2. Briefly stated, the facts giving rise to the dispute referred 
to for the adjudication of this Tribunal are as under :—* 

(i) Shri Banwari La I Soni, hereinafter referred to as the 
workman, was appointed as a Messenger at the Bilaspur 
Branch of the State Bank of India, hereinafter referred 
fo as the Bank, on 8-6-1961, While working as a Messenger 
the workman was promoted as a Cashier-cum-Clerk in 1971. 
Before being absorbed on the post of Cashier-cum-Clerk 

his antecedents had to be verified. The Bank any how came 
to know that the workman was prosecuted on 9-9-1957 for an 
offence under Sec. 379 I.P.C, for comitting a theft of 
Rs, 2[6[- by means of pick-pocketing. On being summarilly 
tried by the Additional District Magistrate, Billaspnr, he was 
sentenced to pay a fine of Rs„ 15/- and in case of default of 
payment of fine to undergo rigorous imprisonment (R.I.) 
for a week, 

tii) Before joining his services with the Bank the 

woikman was requited to give certain declaration. It was 
alleged by the Bank that Shri Banwari Lai Soni before 
joining the Bank gave a declaiation that he was not a previous 

convict of any offence, Since from the records of the trial of 

the workman held in the Bilaspur Court it was found that he 
was convicted of the offence of a theft, the Bank 
acting under Sec, 10 or the Eankinc Regulations, Act 
discontinued his set vices with effect from 5-4-1972. The 
workman therefore appealed to the higher authorities 
of the Bank but all his appeals at diffeierf levels Upto 
the highest level wcie dismissed. The workman accor¬ 
dingly raised a dispute before the Conciliation Officer 
before whom the Bank did not agree to continue 



3332 


I HE GAZETTE OF INDIA : OCTOBER 3, 1981/ASVINA 11, 1903 Part II— Sec 3(ii] 


him in thc Bank scivice. Th c matter was referred to the 
Government of India and finally the Dispute was refeired to 
this Tiihunal for adjudication. In the meantime, the work¬ 
man had also filed a civil suit challenging the order of 
hii termination of service but his suit was dismissed by the 
trial court and by the appellate court also on the ground 
that subject matter of the suit was of the nature of an 
industrial dispute and the Civil Courts had no jurisdiction 
to adjudicate upon it. 

3. In the statement of claim of (he workman, it was staled 
by him that after his appointment in June 8, 1961 he was 
confirmed on 8-12-1961 that after passing certain depart’ 
mental examinations he was granted promotion as the post of 
a Cashier-cuni-clerk; that he was neither tried nor convicted 
of the offence alleged against him; that he was neither given any 
charge-sheet nor any opportunity to defend himself; that his 
services were discontinued without following the prescribed 
proceduie ; that the provisions of Sec. 10 of the Banking 
Regulations Act did not apply to him ; that looking to the 
time that had elapsed between the date of alleged conviction 
and sentence and the date on which his services were dis¬ 
continued the Bank was not justified in invoking th e provisions 
of Banking regulations Act and that on all these grounds he was 
entitled to be rainstated with all benefits of incicmcnts, 
back wages, promotions etc. etc, 

4. In their statement of claim thc Bank management has 
justified the action taken against the workman. It Is stated 
that the workman had iiled a false declaration at the time 
of his initial appointment with the Bank ; that it was only 
after the verification of the workman’s antecedents at the 
time of his promotion as a Cashier-cum-Clerk that it was 
revealed that he was a previous convict of an offence in¬ 
volving moral turpitude; that in view of the specific provi¬ 
sions of the Bank Regulations Act there was no necessity 
either for a domestic enquiry or for an opportunity to be 
given to the workman to defend himself as is generally done 
fn other cases of misconduct while in service and that the 
Bank was fully justified, both on the facts *as well as in law, 
in teiminating the services of thc workman. 

5. The only issue foi decision is this case was the dispute 
referred to for adjudication to this Tribunal. Both the parties 
led oral and documentary evidence in support of their res¬ 
pective contentions. During the comse of the arguments a 
number of points were raised which arc dealt with below. 
In my opinion, Ole following arc the main points to be decid¬ 
ed in this case :— 

1. Whether the workman, Shri Banwari Lai Soni, was 
convicted of the offence of theft in the Court of 
Additional District Magistrate, Bilaspur in Crimi¬ 
nal Case No, 1831| 1957 and was sentenced to pay 
a fine of Rs. 15 or to undergo rigorous imprison¬ 
ment for a week in case of default of payment of 
fine ? 

2. In case the workman was convicted as alleged by 
the Bank then whether the Bank was justified in 
terminating his services by virtue of the provisions 
of Seu. 10 of the Banking Regulations Act without 
holding a regular domestic enquiry? 

3. To what relief are the parties entitled to? 


6, My answers to the aforesaid questions arc as under :— 

Point No 1—The Bank management has proved that it 
was the workman, Shri Banwari Lai Soni who was convicted 
of an offence of theft in Criminal Case No, 1831 [57 in the 
Court of Additional District Magistrate, Bilaspur and was sen¬ 
tenced to pay a fine of Rs. 15 or in default of payment of 
fine t 0 undeigo to one week's rigorous imprisonment. 


Point No 2.—The Bank management was not justified in 
terminating the services of thc workman because of the 
facts and circumstances of the case and also because the 
nature of the offence for which the workman was convicted 
cannot he said to involve any clement of moral turpitude, 


Point No 3._The workman is entitled to be reinstated 

on the post of Cashier-cum-Clerk. But in the circumstances 
of the case, he is not entitled to any benefit of back wages, 
increments, promotions etc. etc. 


7. Reasons:— 

Point No. Hi).—The first point for consideration is whe-

It was, however, said that in the Bank’s record the workman

of Khaparganj, Bilaspur. Furthei according to the workman 
his age as recorded in the Criminal Trial is 22 on the date of 
trial and conviction on 8-9-1957. As against this according 
io him, his age according to the School Leaving Certificate 
Ex. W|16 he was only 17 years because of his date of birth 
being 15-4-1940, These discrepancies appearing in the matter 
of ago in the Criminal Count's judgement and the school Cer¬ 
tificate, os also in the place ot residence in my opinion 
do not help the workman, It is a vvell known fact that when 
the police prosecutes an accused in a Criminal Court the 
age i» mentioned on the basis of approximation only and not 
after verification of the date of birth On this ground, the 
age shown as 22 in the Criminal Court’s Judgment cannot 
be a conclusive factor to held that the accused in that case 
is not the same person as the workman. 

(vi) Regarding the place of lesidence shown as Khapargunj 
in the Criminal Court’s recoid and Gondapara as shown in 
the Bank's record it has to be said that this* also does not 
help th e workman. 

(vii) Evidence pioduced in proceedings before this Tribu¬ 
nal under the Industrial Disputes Act cannot be assessed on 
the same lines on which it is assessed in Criminal cases. 
The trial admittedly was held in the year 1957 and In the 
judgment of the Criminal Court (Ex. M| 6 ) the age and 
place of residence must have been mentioned on the basis 
of th e information given by the accused himself. When the 
Bank had produced reliable and documentry evidence the 
burden was on the workman to examine some person from 
Khaparganj Bilaspur to show that there was some other 
person by the name of Banwari Laf S|o Nand Lai and 
that he could be the accused in the aforesaid criminal case and 
not he. The accused admittedly is a resident of Bilaspur, He 
cannot be completely ignorant of the locality known ns 
Khaparganj and Gondapara. Some residents of any of the two 
places could have come forward and pledged his oath lo 
say that besides the woikman concerned, there was c ome 
other person known as Banwari Lai S]o Nand Lai. 

(vlii) On the question that in the Criminal Court's Judg¬ 
ment (Ex M/ 6 ) Banwari Lai's father name is shown as Nand 
Lai Marwari, but in the Bank's record he i n shown as 
Banwari Ini $|o Nand Lai Sonar, R|o Laxmangarh (Rajas¬ 
than). Soni and Sonar are two synonymous words indicating 
that the person by profession is « goldsmith. Persons from 
Rajasthan aie generally known as Marwaris. This fact is a 
well known tact and requires no evidence to prove it 
Though, the workman 9 a vs that he is Banwari Lai Soni but 
in the seivice sheet of the Bank. Ex, M [8 his name fs re¬ 
corded as Banwari Lai S|o Nand Lai Sonar. In this record 
also his date of birth Is shown as 154-1940, Accordingly 
in my opinion, the workman Shri Banwari Fal S]o^ Shri 
Nand Lai Sonar (also called Soni) and Shri Banwari Lai 
S|o Shri Nand I-al Marwaii shown as an accused in the 
Criminal Court's Tudgment (Ex W [G) are one and the same 
person. As already stated above in the circumstances of this 
cage the burden shifted on the workman to prove that the 
person shown as being tried and convicted in the aforesaid 
criminal case was some other person and not he. This bur¬ 
den has not been discharged by the workman comemed. 
Accordingly, considering the documentary evidence relied 
upon by the Bank, I am not inclined to place any reliant e 
on the oral evidence given by the workman. I accordingly 
held that th* workman Shri Banwari Lai Flo Shri Nand 
Lai Soni R|o Bilaspur who was tried and convicted as conted- 
ed by the Bank Is the same person. Point No. 1 is answered m 
favour of the Bank, 

8 . Point No, 2(i) The second question Is whether Insnlte of 
the workman’s piCvlous conviction for an offience of theft 
hi sc'vices were liable to be discontinued in the light of 
the provisions of Sec. 10 of the Banking Regulations Act. 
Section 10 of the Banking Regulations Act reads as 
under :■—- 

“Prohibition of employment of managing agents and 
rest riel Ions on cerium forms of employment— 

(1) No Banking Cnmnanv— (a) shall employ ir 
be managed bv managing agent; or fbl shall cmnlov 
or continue the employment of rnv person—ri) 
who is or at nnv time has beer?, adjudicated im 
Kob'ent or Ins suspended pnvmenr 01 has com¬ 
pounded with his creditors, or who is, or has been, 
co’^'ic ted by a Criminal Court of an offence in¬ 
volving moral turpitude," 

^27 OT/ft 1 —20 


According to this provision there is a complete prohibition 
for a bank to continue the employment of any person who 
is 01 has been convicted by a Criminal Court of an offence 
involving moral turpitude. This section no doubt lays down 
that :— 

(i) any person before his employment in the Bank if 

convicted by a Criminal Court of an offence involv¬ 
ing moial turpitude; and 

(ii) if while in the employment of the Bank Is convicted 
of an offence by Criminal Court of an offence In¬ 
volving moral turpitude. 

then the Bank can discontinue his employment. Thus 
nny conviction of an offence by a Criminal Court of an off¬ 
ence involving moral turpitude whether before the employ¬ 
ment or while In employment would justify the discontinu¬ 
ance of the services of the employee. However* the convic¬ 
tion must be not for any offence but must be for an offence 
involving moral turpitude. The offence of theft is no doubt 
not a petty offence but whether in a given case the offence 
committed by an accused does or does not have an element 
of moral turpitude will have to be determined on the facts 
and circumstances of each individual case. 

(ii) A theft of petty amount by a person holding a position 
of trust or confidence will indicate an dement of moral 
turpitude but in all cases of theft no inference about such 
an element may be possible, The words “moral turpitude" 
have not been defined. But generally they convey depravity 
rn private and social duties, dishonest motive, depravity in 
moral character etc. etc. In this case the workman was bora on 
15-4-194D (See E\ts. M|16 and M|17, the School Leaving 
Certificates and the Service Sheet Ex. M| 8 . The date of 
conviction is 9-9-1957. The workman was, therefore, only 17 
years old when be was tried and convicted. The nature of the 
offence was not very serious. At the Bilaspur Bus Stand a minor, 
aged 17 years of age, picked the pocket of one Shri Chait 
Ram and dishonestly removed a sum of Rs. 2]6I-. The trial 
was held under the provisions of Sec. 263 of the Code of 
Criminal Procedure 1898. Even under the provisions of that 
Code the accused could, according to the provisions of Sec. 
562, be released on admonition instead of being sentenced. 
The amount involved was a verv petty amount and the ac¬ 
cused was admittedly a minor. All that can be said Is that 
th*' aforesaid art of committing theft was an act of teenage 
delinquency committed by a boy who was not mature and 
was a minor, Considering all these facts and circumstances re¬ 
garding the nature of the offence, the amount involved and 
Ihe age of the offender, It cannot be said that the commission 
of the offence had an element of moral turpitude, A minor 
of 17 years of age, though not a child, but cannot be said 
10 be of such mature understandings ns to be fully conversant 
with his social and moral responsibilities. I am, therefore, 
unable to agree with the Bank that the offence committed by 
the workman in the year 1957 when he was only 17 years 
of ag>e was an offence imolving moral turpitude. 

(ili) It is evident from the Bank’s evidence that when the 
question of promotion and appointment of a workman from 
ihe post of Messenger to the post of Cashler-cum-Clerk 
vns ronsidered thR workman was actually promoted but at 
that time some information was received about his previous 
conviction After verification from the police record as well 
us from the Criminal Court’s record once it was found that 
the workman wn c a previous convict the Bank rushed to the 
conclusion that since he was convicted of an offence involving 
moral Inrpitude, the provisions of Sec, 10 of the Banking 
Regulations Act were fairly and squarely attracted and the 
Bank was hound to discontinue his services, Such a hurried 
conclusion was In my opinion, without any due regard to 
the nature of the offence, the amount involved, the age of 
the accused and other relevant factors. There was no material 
with tlv Bank that after his initial appointment as a Messenger 
in 1961 there was anything in the work and conduct of the 
workman to Justify such a hurried conclusion. On the con¬ 
trary, the decision lo promote him mnst have been taken after 
rwverlainmg all factor* about his moral character and conduct 
wh'le he was in service. In my opinion, therefore, the deci¬ 
sion of the Bank In terminating his services by virtue of the 
provisions of Sec. 10 of the Banking Regulations Act was 
not at all called for. 



3334 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA . OCTOBER 3, 19*1, AS VINA 11, 1903 


[Part 11—Sec. 3^ii)] 


(iv) The con lent ion of tht workman that there waa no 
chaigc-shceL, no domestic enquiry, no opportunity to defend 
him etc. etc, is in my opinion inelevant. Such n couise be- 
comes necessary when the workman while In service is found 
guilty of any miscoodmt or other act 01 comuihsion or omis¬ 
sion justifying in departmental action. Evidently, the Bank 
acted upon the provisions of the Banking Regulations Act and 
stramghtaway passed an order of discontinuance of his ser¬ 
vices, The Bank also appeals to have been led away by the 
fact that while entering the service the workman had given 
a declaration dated 8th June, 1961 (Ex. M|6A) (inadvertantly 
marked Ex. M|6 but now maikcd Ex. M|6A) in which he 
had declared that he was not convicted by a Criminal Court 
of an offence involving moral turpitude. The Bank would 
have been justified if any action had been taken against the 
woikman for Giving such a false declaration but this has not 
been done in this case as the impugned order of discontinuance 
of his set vice is based on the ground of previous conviction 
onTy, 

(v) In the light of the reasons given above, my finding on 
Point No. 2 is that the Bank's management Was not justified 
in teiminuting or discontinuing the services of the workman 
on the ground that he was a previous convict of an offence 
involving moml turpitude. 

9, Point No, 3(i)—The ne,\t question is as to whether the 
woikman is enthled to he leinstatcd on the pod of Cashier- 
curri-Clerk with all the consequential benefits of back wages, 
increments, promotions elc. 

(ji) Admittedly the impugned order was passed in 1972. 
After the conciliation proceedings the woikman had filed a 
Civil Suit No. 1A|78 and 11 A|78 (Ex. W|6 and W|7) This suit 
was dismissed on 7th April, 1978 (vide Ex. W|G) on the giound 
that the Civil Court had no jurisdiction to entertain the suit. 
Before the institution of the suit the workman had been 
approaching the higher authorities of the Bank for reconsidera¬ 
tion of the impugned older, but till 1978 when his suit was 
dismissed he did not avnil of the remedy provided by the 
Industrial Disputes Act. After the dismissal of his suit the 
workman approached the Central Government for a reference 
but his first application is said to have been rejected. It was 
on the second application that the present reference was made 
in October, 1980. The action on the part of the Bank's manage¬ 
ment for discontinuing or terminating the services of the work¬ 
man cannot b$ said to be wholly unjustified. The delay in 
getting the reference made to this Tribunal cannot be whollv 
attributed to Ibc BankV mnnagf-mcot. 'Hie workman himself 
gave a false declaration. The impugned order has been held 
to be unjustified not because the workman was not tried and 
convicted but because of the eiror on the pait of the Bank 
to too literally construe the words "moral turpitude” while 
applying the provisions of Section 10 of the Banking Regula¬ 
tions Act. All these facts and circumstances, in mV opinion* 
do not justify an Older against the Bank for an award of all 
the ha k wages and other consequential benefits Ends or jus¬ 
tice would be met hy directing the Bank to reinstate the 
workman on the post of Cnshier-cum-Clerk for which he was 
selected on promotion. 


ORDER 

Accordingly for th^ reasons given abn\t\ il is diicctcd that 
the Bank shall reinstate the workman on the post of Cashler- 
cum-ricrk. The workman shall not be entitled to any back 
w^cps and other conrenuenlbl benefits from the date of hh 
discon I inua nee till the date of his reinstatement In the cir- 
cnmatnnrcs of the rue, both the parties shall hear their own 
costs' ns incurred, 

7th September, 1981.

of the period during which that factory was subject to the 
further period of one year with effect from the 1st July,

purposes of,—

irfitted under sub-section (1) of section 44 of the 
said Act for the period, or 

(if) ascertaining whether registers and records wer e main¬

and kind being benefits in consideration of which

or

be empowered to—

S.O. 2699.—In exercise of the powers conferred by section 
88 read with section 91A of the Employees’ State Insurance 
Act, 1948 (34 of 1948) and in continuation of the notification 
of the Government of India in the Ministry of Labour No. 
S.O. 1481 dated the 14th May, 1980, the Central Govern¬ 
ment hereby exempts the regular employees of the Hindustan 
Aeronautics Limited (Lucknow Division), Lucknow fiom the 
operation of the said Act for a further period of one year 
with effect from the 1st July, 1980 upto and inclusive of the 
30th June, 1981. 

The above exemption ig subject to The following conditions, 
namely:— 

(1) The aforesaid factory wherein the employees aie em¬ 

ployed shall maintain a register showing the names 
and designations of the exempted employees ; 

(2) Notwithstanding this exemption, the employees shall 

c-mtinue to receive such benefits under the 
said Act to which they migth have become entitled to 
on the basis of the contributions paid prior to the 
date from which exemption granted by this notifi¬ 
cation operates; 

(3) The contributions for the exemp ed period, if already 

paid, shall not be refunded ; 

(4) The employer of the said factory shall submit in 

respect of the piiod during which that factory was 
subject to the operation of the said Act (hereinafter 
referred to as the said period), such returns In such 
form and containing such particulars as were due 
from it in respect of the said period under the 
Employees’ State Insurance (General) Regulations, 
1950; 

(5) Any Inspector appointed by the Corporation under 

uub-section (1) of section 45 of the said Act or 
other official of Iho Corporation authorised in this 
behalf shall, for the purposes of — 

(i) verifying the particulars contained in any lefum 
submitted under sub-section (1) of section 44 for 
the said period ; or 

(li) ascertaining whether registers and records were 
maintained as required by the Employees’ State 
Insurance (General) Regulations, 1950 ft>r the said 
period ; or 

(iii) Ascertaining whether the employees continue to he 

entitled to benefits provided by the employer in cash 
and kind being benefits in consideration of which 
exemption is being granted under this notification; 
or 

(iv) ascertaining whether any of the provisions of the 

Act had been complied with during the neriod 
when such provisions were In force fn relation to 
the said factory. 

be empowered to— 

(a) require the principal or immediate employer tu fur¬ 
nish to him such information as he may consider 
necessary; or

exemption in this case, as tne processing of the application

biotics Limited, Pimpri, Pune, from the operation of the
