S.O. 2708. In exercise of the powers conferred by section 
87 read with section 9lA of the Employees* State Insurance 
Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), the Central Government hereby ex¬ 
empts Antibiotic Plant, Virbhandra, Water In Lake Plant, 
Rishikesh, Biological Plant, Rishikesh and auto repair work¬ 
shop; Rishikesh belonging to M/s. Indian Drug and Pharma¬ 
ceuticals Limited, Rishikesh from the operation of the said Act 
for a period of one year with effect from 24th November, 
1979 upto and inclusive of the 23rd November, 1980. 

2. The above exemption is subject to the following condi¬ 
tions, namely:— 

(1) The employer of the said factory shall submit in res¬ 
pect of the period during which that factory was subject to 
the operation of the said Act (hereinafter referred to as 
the said period), such returns in such form and containing 
such particulars as were due from it In respect of the 
said period under the Employees* State Insurance (General) 
Regulations, 1950; 


(2) Any Inspector appointed by the Corporation under sub¬ 
section (1) of section 45 of the said Act or other official 
of the Corporation authorised in this behalf shall, for the 
purposes of,— 

(i) verifying the particulars contained in any return sub¬ 

mitted under sub-section (1) of section 44 of the 
said Act for the period; or 

(ii) ascertaining whether registers and records were 

maintained as lequired by the Employees' State 
Insurance (General) Regulations, 1950 for jhe said 
period; or 


fiii) asccitaining whether the employees continue to be 
entitled to benefits provided by the employer in 
cash and kind being benefits in consideration of 
which exemption Is being granted under th J s notifica¬ 
tion ; or 

(iv) ascertaining Avhether any of the provisions of the *aid 
Act has been complied with during the period when 
such provisions were in force in relation to the said 
factory; 

be empowered to— 

(a) require the principal or immediate employer to 

furnish to him such information as he may consider 
necessary; or 

(b) enter any factory, establishment, office or other 
premises occupied by such principal or immediate 
employer at any reasonable time and require any 
person found-in-charge thereof to produce to such 
Inspector or other official and allow him to examine 
such accounts, books and other documents relating 
to the employment of persons and payment of 
wages or to furnish to him such information as he 
may consider necessary; or 

(c) examine the principal or immediate employer, his 

agent or servant or any person found in such factory, 
establishment, office or other premises or any per¬ 
son whom the said Inspector or other official has 
reasonable cause to believe to have been an 
employee ; or 

(d) make copies of or take extracts from, any register, 

account book or other document maintained in 
such factory, establishment, office or other premises. 

EXPLANATORY MEMORANDUM 

It has become necessary to give retrospective effect to the 
exemption in this case as the recommendation of the Em¬ 
ployees' State Insurance Corporation for grant of exemption 
was received late. However, it is certified that the grant of 
exemption with retrospective effect will not affect the inte¬ 
rest of anybody adversely,

S O 2717.—In exercise of the poweig conferred by section 
87, read with section 91 A, of the Employees 1 State Insurance 
Act, 1948 (34 of 1948) and in continuation of the notification 
of the Government of India in the Ministry of Labour No

AWARD 

Tins is a reference under S, K> of the l.D. Act, 1947. I he 
Central Government by its order No. L-29U11/2/79 D.1II.B 
daied 15th July, 1980 has referred this dispute lo this 'tribu¬ 
nal for adjudication on the following terms; 

SCHEDULE 

Whether the action of the management ot M/s Pyiucs, 
phosphates and chemicals Limited, F.u, Arnjhoie, 
District Kohtas in refusing to reinstate bhn Mathura 
Prasad, Shuttle Car UperatOr with elleet from 
14-JU-/3 is justified / n not, io whai leliel the con¬ 
cerned workman is entitled 7” 

LRfoie 1 pio^ccd to deal with the ^use ot the panics, 
i would like Lo place certain lacts connected wall u. un 
at anout ll) A.M. biiu burajtnli v houbey* becuruy 
inspector oi pyrites, Ptiosphaies and Chemicals i-imiKd 
received information. Irorn AmjUore Police that they had 
information dial some materials sioicn liom die company 
weie tying in the house ot Stm Mathuia Piasad ot village 
iciKup. iqc police requested Mm choubcy io arrange a 
Jeep so that a raid could be conducted, Sim Chou bey uiong 
with sOjpc security men went to the police station and item 
thcie the police Inspector, Amihore went to the house oi bhn 
Mathura Prasad in the village Tclkup and leached tiiere at 
12 Noon on 5-2-73. Shri Mathura Prasad was present in the 
house. The police searched the house and iournl one Angle, 
One pm, Fan belt and some healings together will) other 
materials which arc used in Shudlc Car. The materials were 
seized by the police and a case was started againsi Shu Mathura 
Prasad. The articles were identified as belonging to the com' 
patiy by Shri D* N. Singh, Mechanical Foreman. The police 
after investigation submitted charge-sheet against Shri 
Mathuia Prasad. The Dial Magistrate passed his order oi 
conviction and sentence. But on appeal Shu Mathura Prasad 
was acquitted. In the meantime Pyrites, Phosphates and Che¬ 
micals Ltd. had started departmental proceeding against Slni 
Mathura Prasad. In the domestic enquiry Shri Mathura Pra¬ 
sad was found guilty of misconduct The employers considci- 
ed the enquiry leport and passed an order of dismissal* 

3. An'industrial dispute was raised by the General Secre¬ 
tary, Amjhore Khan Mazdoor Union for the 1 cason that the 
employers had refused to reinstate Shri Mathura Prasad 
w.e.f. 14-10-73 in view of the order of acquital passed by 
the Appellate Court. On failure of conciliation this refeicnco 
has been made. 

4. Shri Mathuia Prasad, Shuttle Car operatoj has taken The 
plea in this reference that at the time of raid he was not 
piesent in his house as he was on duly till 2.00 P.M. The 
lime of raid was said to bo at 1.00 P.M, The recovery of 
materials from his house is also said to be fabrication by 
the company’s officials in conspiiHcy with Amjhore Police* A 
further plea has been taken that it was not established that 
the recovered maleiml belong to the company. Further more, 
no report of theft of materials had been made befotc the 
police. 

5. The management has admitted that there was no report 
to the police about theft of materials of the company. This 
raid was conducted by the police with the help of security 
staff of the company as the police had information that 
certain stolen articles of <he comp my were in the bouse of 
SHri Mathura Prasad. The articles recovered consisted of 
parts of Shuttle Car, such as V. Belts, Axile Pins, Inner Link 
of Conveyoi. Chain, Bearings and some serviceable parts 
identified by the Mechdnieal Foreman of the company. A 
charge-sheet dated 14115^-73 was issued against Shri 
Mathura Prasad who submitted a written icply. The reply 
was not considered to be satisfactory and domestic enquiry 
was ordered to be made. The enquiry started on 12-3-73 and 



3356 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : OCTOBER 


continued upto 16-7-73 in which. Shri Mftlhuru Prasad got 
every opportunity to defend himself. The witnesses tor the 
management were cross-examined and a defence witness was 
also examined. The enquiry officer found that the charge 
levelled against Shri Mathura Prasad had been proved. His 
jcpoit was considered by the ^ Chief Mining Engineer who 
was the competent authority, and he ordered Shi i Mathura 
Piasad lo be dismissed from service. A show cause was 
also invited with regard to the proposed punishment bcfoic 
finally ordering his dismissal Shri Mathura ITasad was 
dismissed w.e.f. 14-10-73. According to the management 
mere acquittal by the Appellate Conn is not enough to oidei 
reinstatement of a workman. 

6. Before 1 proceed to discuss the case on merits, 1 would 
like to point out that at the commencement of the healing 
Shri Lalit Barman representing the workman conceded tnar 
he would not challenge the jairness and propriety of the 
domestic enquiry. It was, therefore, lccorded that ine do¬ 
mestic enquiry was fair and proper. The parties agreed to 
admit into evidence the documents of both sides with regard 
to domestic enquiry, the First Information Repoit of the 
case and the two judgments of the Trial Court and Ihe 
Appellate Cotut. This case was therefore heard on mails. 

7. 1 need not discuss the case laws lesting on the ellect of 
judgment ol acquittal of ciimmal court in the matter of dismis¬ 
sal of the workman. The concerned workman has been 
dismissed in a departmental enquiry in connection with tne 
stealing of mutcnals of the company and for putting the com¬ 
pany to loss. Such materials .vcrc recovered by the police 
from his house in presence of the security personnel. Ihe 
articles recovered were identified to be the property of the 
company. The Trial Magistiate held that th& concerned work¬ 
man was found to be in possession of stolen properly, and 
hence recorded the order of punishment and sentence. The 
concerned workman was acquitted by the Appellate Court on 
the ground that the search was not conducted according to 
law, and that there was no satisfactory evidence to show that 
the articles recovered were the stolen property. 

8. The position is clear that for Ihe purpose of a cwminai 
trial it was necessary to •-how that the property recovered was 
a stolen property for which the Appellate Court found that 
there was no satisfactory evidence. With regard to the recovrey 
itself the learned Appellate Court found that the piovision 
of S. 103 Cr. P,C. were not properly observed and therefore 
the search was not according to law. On the hasis of those 
two facts there was bound to be acquittal. But such acquittal 
cannot be said lo be a dean acquittal because apparently the 
concerned woikman was given a benefit of doubt. If it was 
a case of clean acquittal there could be a good deal of force 
in saying that the company was bound to respect the court 
order and to icinstaie the workman. But in this case the 
company felt that there was no clean acquittal and so they did 
not Reinstate the concerned workman merely on the basis 
of acquittal by the Appellate Court. 

9. It has been conceded by both sides that in a case tike 
this it must always be considered as to whether Ihe dismissal 
is justified because if it is not justified there is bound lo be 
reinstatement irrespective of Lhe decision of the criminal couit. 

It has also been conceded that in a trial of a criminal case 
the law and facts of a particular case have to be considered 
from the point of view of criminal intent of an accused But 
in a disciplinary proceeding the case has to be judged from the 
point of view of misconduct as laid down in the standing 
order of the company. It is therefore necessary lo look into 
the evidence adduced at the time of domestic cnquiiy in order 
to ascertain whether the charge framed against the concerned 
workman has been proved, 

10. Befo c we proceed to discuss the evidence, let us 
acquaint old selves with the documents produced in this case. 
The management filed 16 documents and they are Exts, Ml 
to M16. Ext. Ml is a copy of .he charge sheet dated 15-2-73, 
Ext, M2 is the reply to the charge-sheet. Ext. M3 is the 
letter of appointment of enquiry officer Shri S. K. Rajan 
under manager, * Ext. M4 Is an office order cancelling the 
appointment of Shi i Rajan, enquiry officer and appointment of 
Shri H. Sanyal, under manager. Fxt, M5 is a corrigendum, 
fn the charge-sheet, Ext. Ml Shri Mathura Sao was men¬ 
tioned and it was corrected through this conigendum Shri 
Mathura Prasad. Ext, M6 is l list containing the proceeding 
of enquiry having 18 sheets. Ext. M7 is a copy of the list 


i, 1981/ASVINA II, 1903 [Part 11-Sec. 3(ii) 


of stolen materials. Ext. MS is a copy of a letter dated 3rd 
February, 1973 addressed to tho Police siatjon, Amjhore under 
which some materials have been stolen troni the garage and 
workshop of tho company on 31-1-73. Ext. M9 is a letter 
by Shri Mathura Prasad addressed to the enquiry officer under 
which certain documents were demanded for being produced

The concerned workman being a shuttle car operator woikcd

It was within his knowledge os a shuttle car operator that 
ihe shuttle car parts of the company were in hia house, 11 u 
was brought by somebody in his house he had no reason 
to keep them in his house and he should have returned those 
to the company in order to show his bona fide. The position 
is that it has been established by the management that the 
articles recovered from the house of the concerned workman 
were parts of the company's machinery. Obviously, the le- 
moval of the parts from the company’s premise^ and re¬ 
covery of the articles from the house of the concerned work¬ 
man would go to show that the company had to bear a loss 
or damage on account of the act of the concerned workman. 
Now, this is precisely the object of the ch me which had been 
fiarned against the concerned workman. It is not Ihe object 
of the departmental proceed mg to establish a case of theft, 
but to establish misconduct which the management has no 
doubt established in this case, 

1A Thus having considered the vaiious aspects of the case, 
I hold that the action of the management of M/s. Pyrites, 
Phosphates and Chemicals Limited, P,0, Amjhorc, District 
Rohtas in refusing to te-instate Shri Mathura Piasad, Shuttle 
Car Operator with effect from 14-10-73 is justified. Conse¬ 
quently, the concerned workman, Shri Mathura Prasad is not 
entitled to any relief. 

This is my award.
